The Commander of the Believers related that the Messenger of Allah (ص) would awaken his household during the last ten nights of Ramadan, tighten his waistband, and pass those nights standing in prayer. Jafar ibn Muhammad explained that "tightening his waistband" means that he withdrew from his wives.

---

**On the Sunnah and Supererogatory Fasting, from Majlis al-Bayan**

Fasting falls into four kinds: obligatory fasting, Sunnah fasting, forbidden fasting, and optional fasting.

The obligatory kind has seven divisions:

1. The fast of the month of Ramadan, observed every year.
2. The fast of two consecutive months for one who deliberately breaks a fast on one of its days and cannot find a slave to free.
3. The fast of two consecutive months for one who kills a believer by mistake, when he cannot find a slave to free while paying the blood money to the family.
4. The fast of two consecutive months in the expiation of zihar, for one who cannot find a slave to free.
5. The fast of three days in expiation for a violated oath, for one who can find neither food, nor clothing, nor a slave to free.
6. The fast of one who joins Umrah to Hajj in the tamattu pilgrimage when he cannot find a sacrificial offering — which amounts to ten days.
7. The fast undertaken by vow.

The Sunnah kind has twelve divisions:

1. The fast of itikaf
2. The fast of Shaban
3. Fasting one Thursday in every month
4. Fasting the three white days
5. Fasting Friday joined with the Thursday before it or the Saturday after it, without singling out Friday alone
6. The fast in the manner of Dawud (ع) — one day of fasting followed by one day of breaking the fast — which is the most rigorous form of voluntary fasting
7. The fast of the day of Arafah, for one who is not a pilgrim
8. The fast of the boy when he reaches nine years of age
9. The fast that serves as expiation for hunting
10. The fast that serves as expiation for shaving the head

The forbidden kind has ten divisions:

1. The fast of Eid al-Fitr
2. The fast of Eid al-Nahr
3–5. The fasts of the three days of Tashriq
6. The fast of wisal — joining two or more days together without breaking the fast between them
7. Fasting in perpetuity
8. Fasting while menstruating
9. Fasting by a vow made in disobedience
10. Fasting in doubt, since fasting is not permitted except upon certainty

The optional kind has twelve divisions, in each of which a person is free to choose: he may fast, or he may break his fast. These are:

1. The fast of Rajab
2. The fast of certain days of Shawwal after Eid al-Fitr, during which the reward of Ramadan is extended
3. The fast of the nine days of Dhu al-Hijjah — the days of the ten
4. The fast of the day of Ashura, observed by refraining from food and drink without the belief that such a fast is superior, since that abstention resembles mourning more than devotion
5. The fast of the month of Muharram, which is one of the sacred months
6. The fast of the sick person and the traveler, upon whom lies the obligation to make up a number of other days, whether they fasted or broke their fast
7. The fast of Monday
8. The fast of permission — meaning that a woman may not fast voluntarily except with her husband's permission, a bondsman may not fast voluntarily except with his master's permission, and a guest may not fast voluntarily except with his host's permission

Among the fasts, too, is the fast of withholding speech, of which Allah Almighty informed us in the account of Maryam, in His words: *"Eat and drink and be comforted; and if you see any human being, say: I have vowed to the Most Merciful a fast, so I will not speak this day to any human."* He commanded her to eat, to drink, and to speak — the command to speak being carried in the words *"so say: I have vowed"* — and this shows that the speech she was forbidden was some speech and not all of it, just as one may address Allah Almighty in intimate supplication during the prayer while all other speech is withheld, as has been discussed in the chapters on prayer.

The Messenger of Allah (ص) did not bind himself to any prayer that was not prescribed. The Imams of his progeny, however, did bind themselves to certain prayers and directed their followers to observe them by way of encouragement and persuasion — not as a binding obligation that may not be left, and not on the level of the obligatory prayer. In the same way, the Messenger of Allah (ص) bound himself, in the latter part of his life, to fasting two months of the year beyond the month of Ramadan; and likewise the Imams of his progeny bound themselves to that and urged their followers toward it by way of exhortation and encouragement, not as something obligatory that may not be abandoned.

It has been narrated from the Imams of his progeny that the Messenger of Allah (ص) would fast until it was said of him that he never broke his fast, then break his fast until it was said of him that he never fasted. His practice moved through several forms:

- He fasted the months of Muharram, Rajab, and Shaban.
- He fasted one day and broke the fast the next.
- He fasted Monday and Thursday.
- He fasted the white nights — the thirteenth of the month and the two days following it. These nights are called white because the moon is visible from the first of the night to its last, as though the darkness itself were made white by its light.

His practice then settled into this: he fasted Shaban and three days in every month — a Thursday at the beginning of the month, meaning its first Thursday; a Thursday at its end, meaning its last Thursday; and a Wednesday in its middle, the Wednesday lying nearest to the midpoint from either side. Those two months carry a reward equal to that of the obligatory fast, and it was upon this practice that he (ص) was taken.

It has been narrated from Abu Abdullah Jafar ibn Muhammad that he would fast Rajab, Shaban, and Ramadan, joining them together. He said that Nuh (ع) boarded the Ark on the first day of Rajab and commanded those with him of the jinn and mankind to fast it. He said: whoever fasts a month of Rajab, the Fire is set from him the distance of a year's journey; if he fasts it two years, the Fire recedes from him the distance of two years; and so it continues, until when he fasts it seven, the seven gates of the Fire are shut against him; if he fasts it eight, the eight gates of Paradise are opened to him; if he fasts it ten, his request is granted; if he fasts it fifteen, it is said to him, "Begin your works anew"; and whoever adds more, Allah adds to him.

Abu Jafar Muhammad ibn Ali was asked about the fast of the day of Ashura and said that it was before the fast of Ramadan was made obligatory. Abu Abdullah Jafar ibn Muhammad said that the Ark came to rest upon Mount Judi on the day of Ashura, and so Nuh commanded those with him to fast that day. He said that it is the day on which Allah Almighty accepted the repentance of Adam and Hawwa; the day on which the sea was parted for the Children of Israel and Pharaoh was drowned; the day on which Ibrahim (ع) was born; the day on which Allah Almighty accepted the repentance of the people of Yunus (ع); the day on which Isa ibn Maryam (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ) was born; the day on which Allah Almighty honored al-Husayn (ع) with martyrdom; and the day on which the Qaim from his progeny shall rise.

Imam al-Muizz li-Din Allah (ع) said that the day of Ashura is a day that Allah, His Messenger, and His friends have magnified, on account of the merit and the proof it contains. The enemies of Allah's friends — from the Umayyads and the progeny of Marwan — magnified it for a different reason entirely: for what they had gained within it by violating the sanctities of Allah, violating the religion of Allah, and killing the sons of the Messenger of Allah (ص). They placed it in the station of gladness and gloating rather than the station of seeking forgiveness and righteous works, turning away from the Sunnah of Allah and the Sunnah of His Prophet concerning it, and persisting in what Allah has forbidden and in disobedience to Him.

From the Imams it has been transmitted that they held fasting the day of Arafah to be beloved — for whoever knows its virtue — with one exception: the person who is at the standing place and fears that fasting would weaken him for the standing itself. They also disliked singling out Friday for fasting, unless it is fasted together with another day, whether the day before it or the day after.

Beyond the zakat that seals the fast, the fast itself carries its own precise rulings. The Imams drew careful lines around three distinct situations:

- when a person fasts
- when he refrains from fasting
- when fasting is not permitted at all

The Master said that whoever fasts on Friday fasts as though he had fasted the entire span between two Fridays — meaning the day of the week arrives and he joins it to another, rather than singling Friday out on its own. He also said he would not accept a supererogatory fast from anyone who still had an obligatory fast outstanding; the obligation had to be discharged first.

A concession runs in the other direction: whoever has begun a voluntary fast may break it without any obligation to make it up, provided the sun has not yet passed its zenith. Should he break it after the sun has declined, he must then make up that day.

The Imams further forbade fasting on:

- the day of al-Fitr
- the day of al-Adha
- the three days of al-Tashreeq

They likewise forbade al-wasal in fasting — the practice of joining two days or more together without breaking the fast between them.

> أُحِلَّ لَكُمْ لَيْلَةَ ٱلصِّيَامِ ٱلرَّفَثُ إِلَىٰ نِسَآئِكُمْ

From fasting the teaching turns to itikaf, the retreat that fasting makes possible. Itikaf is the act of remaining and residing within a mosque; the akif is the one who stays there. Of it Allah, mighty and majestic, said, "the one who remains there and the visitor are equal," and He, blessed and exalted, said, "And do not engage in relations with them while you are in retreat in the mosques" [al-Baqarah: 187] ﴿ وَلَا تُبَاشِرُوهُنَّ وَأَنْتُمْ عَاكِفُونَ فِى الْمَسَاجِدِ﴾ المساجد﴾, the reference being to women. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ used to observe itikaf in the last ten days of Ramadan, binding himself to the mosque by night and by day. He would say that itikaf in those ten days equals in reward two Hajj and two Umrahs. Through those nights he remained standing in prayer, and each night he would bathe between the two evening prayers. He observed itikaf in the first ten days of Ramadan, then in the middle ten the following year, and then in the last ten the year after that.

Ali said that whoever undertakes itikaf ought to keep to the mosque night and day, giving himself over within it to prayer, to the recitation of the Quran, and to the remembrance of Allah, mighty and majestic. He should not occupy himself with conversation except where it cannot be avoided. He should neither sell nor buy, nor recite poetry, nor attend a funeral, nor visit the sick, nor enter a house, nor seclude himself with a woman, nor speak in coarse or whispered speech, nor lay a hand upon anyone. Whatever he withholds from speaking with people is better for him.

It was narrated from Jafar ibn Muhammad that he said, "There is no itikaf except with fasting, and no itikaf except in a mosque in which the congregational prayer is held. The one in itikaf does not pray in his house, does not receive women, does not sell and does not buy, and does not leave the mosque except for a need he cannot forgo — and once he leaves for it he does not sit until he returns." The same rulings apply to a woman in itikaf, except that should she begin to menstruate her itikaf is nullified and she departs from the mosque. The least duration of itikaf is three days.

## 8. The Journey to the House: Pilgrimage

> وَأَذِّن فِى ٱلنَّاسِ بِٱلْحَجِّ يَأْتُوكَ رِجَالًۭا وَعَلَىٰ كُلِّ ضَامِرٍۢ يَأْتِينَ مِن كُلِّ فَجٍّ عَمِيقٍۢ

The greatest of the journeys undertaken for the sake of Allah is the Hajj. Allah Almighty said: "And proclaim the Hajj among the people; they will come to you on foot and on every lean camel; they will come from every distant mountain pass" [al-Hajj: 27].

It was narrated from Jafar ibn Muhammad (ع), from his father, that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: When Allah Almighty commanded Ibrahim (ع) to call the people to Hajj, he ascended Mount Safa, placed his finger in his ear, and called out as Allah Almighty had commanded him. His call was heard by those in the east and in the west — even those yet unborn in the loins of their fathers — and they answered him. Whoever fulfills the Hajj once has answered once, whoever fulfills it twice has answered twice, and whoever fulfills it more times has answered accordingly.

Jafar ibn Muhammad said: "From this the Talbiyah was made."

The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: "Hajj is the jihad of every weak person." He also said: "Whoever circumambulates this House seven times and prays two rakahs, the best of prayers, will be forgiven." When he stood on the eve of Arafah, he faced the people and said three times: "Welcome to the delegation of Allah!" Then he said: "Those who ask shall be given, and those who fall short in this world shall be compensated; for every thousand dirhams spent, good deeds are written for them. O people, shall I not give you glad tidings? This evening Allah Almighty commanded His angels, and they descended to the earth, and He said to them: O My angels, look upon My servants, dusty and disheveled, who have come to Me from the ends of the earth. Do you know what they ask? They will say: Our Lord, You know best. And He will say: They ask Me for forgiveness, and I bear witness to you that I have forgiven them."

Hajj carries the reward of the Garden, and Umrah is expiation for every sin. He (ع) said: "Alternate between Hajj and Umrah, for they negate poverty and sins and bring sustenance." He said: "There are sins that cannot be forgiven except at Arafat."

Concerning the Hajj caravan, he said: "Do not turn them away" — meaning that their reward is written for them whether or not it is concealed; no veil is lifted from them unless their evil deeds are first removed. When they have fulfilled their rites, it is said to them: "You have built a foundation, so do not demolish it. What has passed is enough for you, so do well in what is to come."

The Prophet ﷺ said: "Whoever desires this world and the next, let him come to this House, for whatever a servant has asked of his Lord, Allah will grant him some of it or store it for him." Then he said: "O people, you must perform Hajj and Umrah and alternate between them, for they wash away sins as water washes away grime, and they negate poverty as fire removes the dross of iron."

Ali (ع) used to say that the righteous man, once he has completed the Hajj, takes up his works anew — for what came before has already been forgiven. Abu Jafar Muhammad ibn Ali (ع) traced back to the Prophet ﷺ that he said: "Pilgrims are of three kinds, and the best in portion is the man who has been forgiven for his past and future sins. Next is the man who has been forgiven for his past sins and begins his works afresh. The least of them is the man who has merely safeguarded his family and his wealth."

The pilgrim must inevitably sustain some loss in wealth, whether through the exertion of the journey itself or through a shortfall in provisioning. Whoever suffers loss in either wealth or family while on Hajj has forfeited one of the two rewards — yet he is forgiven for what he has done and what he has deferred, his former sins are pardoned, and he is called to fear Allah in what lies ahead.

Jafar ibn Muhammad said: when Allah Almighty sent Adam (ع) down to the earth, Adam wandered to the House just as he had seen the angels circling the Throne, and he called out to his Lord asking for forgiveness. The call came: "Allah has forgiven you." He said: "O Lord, and my descendants?" And the call came: "O Adam, whoever among your descendants comes to this place bearing his sin as you have borne yours, Allah will forgive him."

Jafar ibn Muhammad said: "The guarantee of the believing pilgrim rests with Allah Almighty — that even if he sins after returning to his family, he remains under Allah's protection for seventy nights." He said concerning the words of Allah Almighty, "Purify My House for those who circumambulate it, those who remain in devotion, and those who bow and prostrate" [al-Baqarah: 125]: "When Allah Almighty revealed that, He sent down upon the Kabah one hundred and seventy mercies:

- sixty for those who circumambulate it,
- fifty for those who remain in devotion,
- forty for those who worship within it, and
- twenty for those who behold it."

A man asked Abu Abdullah Jafar ibn Muhammad which was better: voluntary Hajj or voluntary charity. The questioner kept returning to charity and its virtue, and Abu Abdullah (ع) kept answering that Hajj is better, repeating this several times, until he turned toward the mountain of Abu Qubays and said: "Even if you were to give in charity its weight in gold and silver, you would not attain the reward of Hajj." Then he said: "Is there in charity a circumambulation of the House? Is there in charity a standing at Arafah? Is there in charity anything of the rites?" The questioner said no. He said: "Then Hajj is better."

Speaking on the words of God Almighty, "That they may witness benefits for them" [al-Hajj: 28], the Master said that these are benefits for them in their religion. He said: "When the believing servant sets out from his home as a pilgrim, he takes no step, and his mount carries him no stage, except that a good deed is written for him by it, an evil deed is erased from him by it, and he is raised a degree by it. When he stands at Arafah, though his sins were as numerous as the dust of the earth, they are erased from him. When he casts the pebbles, his rank is raised, to be fulfilled for him on the Day of Resurrection. When he shaves his head, not a hair falls from him but God makes its place a light for him on the Day of Resurrection. Whatever he spends is written for him. And when he circumambulates the House, he returns as on the day his mother bore him" — meaning sound and free of sins.

The Master then recited the words of God Almighty: "Whoever hastens his departure in two days, there is no sin on him, and whoever delays, there is no sin on him, for the one who fears Allah" [al-Baqarah: 203]. He said: "No one stands upon those mountains, whether righteous or wicked, and calls upon God except that He answers him. As for the righteous, he is answered in his Hereafter, and as for the other, he is answered in this world."

> حِجُّ ٱلْبَيْتِ مَنِ ٱسْتَطَاعَ إِلَيْهِ سَبِيلًۭا ۚ وَمَن كَفَرَ فَإِنَّ ٱللَّهَ غَنِىٌّ عَنِ ٱلْعَلَمِينَ

On the obligation of Hajj, Allah Almighty said: "And Hajj to the House is a duty owed to Allah by the people, for whoever is able to find a way to it; and whoever disbelieves, then indeed Allah is free of need of the worlds" [Al Imran: 97].

When this verse was revealed, the Prophet ﷺ remained silent as the people asked, "O Messenger of Allah, is it every year?" They put the question to him twice more, and at last he said: "No; and had I said yes, it would have become obligatory." Allah Almighty then revealed: "O you who believe, do not ask about things that, if disclosed to you, would distress you" [al-Maidah: 101].

Ali spoke to the words of Allah Almighty, "And whoever disbelieves, then indeed Allah is free of need of the worlds" [Al Imran: 97], saying: "This is said of the one who abandons Hajj while he is able to perform it."

Jafar ibn Muhammad was asked about the words of Allah Almighty, "for whoever is able to find a way to it" [Al Imran: 97] — specifically, what constitutes that ability. He asked the questioner what people say on the matter, and the man replied that they say it means having provisions and a mount. Abu Abdullah said: "Abu Jafar was asked about this, and he said: The people would be ruined then. If a man who has nothing but provisions and a mount, with no sustenance left for his family besides that, were compelled to set out with it and leave them nothing, they would indeed be ruined." When asked, then, what the ability truly is, he said: "The ability to make the journey, sufficient provision for it, the presence of what sustains one's dependents, and the security of the road. Has not Allah Almighty made zakat obligatory only upon the one who possesses two hundred dirhams?"

The Master was asked about a man who postpones Hajj for no reason beyond a trade that occupies him or a debt he owes. He said: "He has no excuse. It is not fitting for him to postpone the Hajj, and if he dies, he has abandoned one of the laws of Islam."

He said: "Whoever dies without having performed the obligatory Hajj, not prevented by a pressing need, nor by an illness that makes the journey unbearable, nor by a ruler who bars him — let him die a Jew or a Christian."

He said: "Hajj is obligatory upon a person once in a lifetime."

It was said to him: "And whoever abandons it while able, until he dies?"

He said: "He is among those of whom Allah Almighty said, *And We will gather him on the Day of Resurrection blind.*"

It was said: "Blind?"

He said: "Yes — blind to the path of truth and good."

He said that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: "If my ummah were to abandon this House and cease to make for it, they would not be granted respite."

He was asked about a child taken on Hajj. He said: "That does not suffice for him; Hajj becomes obligatory upon him when he reaches maturity." He was asked about one who performs Hajj without knowledge of this matter, and then Allah favors him with that knowledge. He said: "His Hajj suffices him, though it would be more beloved to me that he perform Hajj again. If, however, he was a Nasib — holding to enmity — and performed Hajj in that state, and then Allah favored him with knowledge, Hajj is upon him again when he is able."

Of the owned servant who performs Hajj, he said: "It suffices him for as long as he remains owned. If he is freed, Hajj becomes obligatory upon him when he is able, for no obligation of Hajj rests upon him while he is still owned." The same applies to a mother whose master takes her on Hajj: it does not suffice for her.

A man may not prevent his wife from performing the obligatory Hajj, provided she has a mahram to travel with her, provision to spend, and the road is secure. He said: "The divorced woman may perform Hajj during her waiting period if she wishes."

If a man is insolvent and another performs Hajj on his behalf, and he later becomes able, Hajj remains upon him.

> وَلَا تَحْلِقُوا۟ رُءُوسَكُمْ حَتَّىٰ يَبْلُغَ ٱلْهَدْىُ مَحِلَّهُۥ

He was asked about Umrah and said: "It is obligatory, as Hajj is obligatory," and he recited the words of Allah Almighty: "And complete the Hajj and Umrah for Allah" [al-Baqarah: 196]. He said: "Their completion is their performance." He held that a man may bring forward whichever of the two he wishes — Hajj or Umrah — and whoever wishes may join them together. Whoever joins them and drives the offering forward does not leave the state of ihram until he has sacrificed; that is the qarin, because Allah Almighty says: وَلَا تَحْلِقُوا۟ رُءُوسَكُمْ حَتَّىٰ يَبْلُغَ ٱلْهَدْىُ مَحِلَّهُۥ — "Do not shave your heads until the offering reaches its place." Whoever joins them without an offering is performing tamattu: upon arriving in Mecca he performs tawaf and say, then leaves ihram — that is the Umrah — and subsequently enters ihram for Hajj from Mecca. Allah Almighty said: "Then whoever enjoys Umrah until the Hajj, then whatever offering is feasible." Abu Abdullah said: "A sheep, or more than that."

As for what one who intends Hajj must observe, Allah Almighty said: "Hajj is during well-known months, so whoever has made Hajj obligatory upon himself therein, let there be no lewdness nor wickedness nor quarreling in Hajj" [al-Baqarah: 197]. Abu Jafar Muhammad ibn Ali said that the well-known months are Shawwal, Dhu al-Qidah, and Dhu al-Hijjah, and Hajj may be made obligatory in none but these. The act of making Hajj obligatory upon oneself is accomplished by any one of three things:

- the Talbiyah
- the ishar
- the taqlid

Whichever of these the one intending Hajj performs, he has made Hajj obligatory upon himself. Lewdness is intercourse; wickedness is lying, cursing, and quarreling; and quarreling is a man's saying "No, by Allah" and "Yes, by Allah," and boasting.

Ali ibn al-Husayn (ع) said to Abu Khalid al-Wasiti, counseling him when he wished to perform Hajj: "O Abu Khalid, when you set out for Mecca, God willing, hold to the fear of Allah, remember Him abundantly, and speak little except in good — for the completion of Hajj and Umrah lies in a man's guarding his tongue. Allah Almighty said: *No lewdness, no wickedness, no quarreling in Hajj.* Lewdness is intercourse; wickedness is lying, cursing, and quarreling; and quarreling is a man's saying 'No, by Allah' and 'Yes, by Allah,' and boasting. Hold to scrupulousness, which restrains you from disobedience to Allah; to forbearance, by which you master your anger; and to good companionship with those who travel with you. There is no power except by Allah."

Abu Abdullah Jafar ibn Muhammad said to a man he was counseling: "When you go out as a pilgrim, hold to the fear of Allah, to forbearance, to scrupulousness, and to good companionship with those who accompany you — for Abu Jafar Muhammad ibn Ali said: 'No account is taken of one who makes for this House if he does not possess three qualities:

1. Scrupulousness that restrains him from disobedience to Allah
2. Forbearance by which he masters his anger
3. Good companionship for those who travel with him'"

Abu Abdullah Jafar ibn Muhammad said to a man who intended to perform Hajj, counseling him: "When you depart from your home for Hajj or Umrah, make the supplication of relief and say: There is no god but Allah, the Forbearing, the Generous; there is no god but Allah, the Most High, the Great; glory be to Allah, Lord of the seven heavens and Lord of the seven earths and Lord of the Throne; and praise be to Allah, Lord of the worlds. O Allah, open before me in this journey of mine with good and seal it for me with good, and preserve me from every creature whose forelock You hold, for You, my Lord, are upon a straight path. In the name of Allah I have set out, in the name of Allah I enter, and for the sake of Allah I journey. O Allah, I place before You my forgetfulness and my haste. In the name of Allah and by the will of Allah — whatever of this journey of mine I remember or forget — O Allah, You are the One whose help is sought over all affairs, the Companion in travel and the Successor over family, wealth, and children. O Allah, make our journey easy for us, fold up the earth for us, and let us travel through it in obedience to You and obedience to Your Messenger. O Allah, set right our affairs for us, bless us in what You have provided us, and guard us from the torment of the Fire."

When setting out upon the road, there is a way to place oneself in Allah's keeping from the very first step. When one sets one's leg among the travelers, the words are: "In the name of Allah, and by Allah, and Allah is the Greatest." Once settled into the journey, one praises Allah seven times, glorifies Him seven times, magnifies Him seven times, says "La ilaha ill Allah" seven times, and then says:

"Praise be to Allah who guided us to Islam and honored us with Muhammad and his family. Glory be to the One who has subjected this to us, for we could not have subdued it ourselves, and indeed to our Lord we shall return. Praise be to Allah, Lord of the worlds. O Allah, You are the Carrier upon the back and the Helper in affairs. O Allah, grant us the best of news, and lead us to Your forgiveness and Your pleasure. O Allah, no bird flies but by Your leave, and no good comes but from You, and none else protects. O Allah, I seek refuge in You from the hardships of travel, from the distress of turning back, and from an ill return to family, wealth, and children. O Allah, I ask You in this journey of mine for piety, for contentment, and for deeds that please You. O Allah, cut short the distance for me and make it easy, and set aright my affairs and leave me well among my family."

And upon returning, the words spoken in that return are: "We are those who repent, who worship, who praise, returning to our Lord, giving thanks, devoted, and to Him we return."

On entering the city of the Prophet (ص), the Commander of the Believers (ع) declared in his sermon that the Messenger of Allah (ص) had proclaimed Madinah sacred between the mountains of Ayr and Thawr. Whoever commits an unlawful act there, or shelters one who does, brings upon himself the curse of Allah, the angels, and all people, and Allah will accept from him neither obligatory nor supererogatory acts.

Jafar ibn Muhammad stated that both flanks of Madinah are sanctified. When asked what those two flanks were, he replied that they are whatever the free woman enclosed within her garment — meaning what the Messenger of Allah (ص) had forbidden: disturbing the inhabitants, hunting its game, and cutting its trees.

Before entering the city, a ritual bath is recommended. The visit begins at the Mosque of the Prophet (ص). There one stands at his grave, offers prayers upon him and his family, and testifies to his prophethood and his message. One prays whatever one is able, then prays two rakahs before sitting at the pillar that stands forward on the side of the grave, from the corner of the grave — for that is the place nearest the head of the Prophet (ص). Then one rises, approaches the grave, and says:

"I bear witness that there is no god but Allah alone, with no partner; and I bear witness that Muhammad is His servant and messenger; and I testify that you are the Messenger of Allah, truly; and I testify that you are Muhammad ibn Abd Allah; and I testify that you conveyed the message of your Lord and gave sincere counsel to your nation, and that you strove in the way of Allah, and that you worshipped Allah until certainty came to you, and you fulfilled what was due upon you. This is the clear truth. May Allah reward you with the best of rewards. O Allah, bless Your servant and messenger Muhammad, as You have described him, as befits his faithfulness and the best of Your creation, and bless the family of Muhammad with the best, most complete, and most beautiful blessings, as You prayed upon one of Your prophets, Your purified ones, and the people of dignity, for You are Glorious and Generous."

O Allah, bless Muhammad and the family of Muhammad as You blessed Noah in the worlds; and bless Muhammad and the family of Muhammad as You blessed Moses and Aaron; and bless Muhammad and the family of Muhammad as You blessed Ibrahim and the family of Ibrahim, for You are the Praised, the Glorious. O Allah, whatever need I have of You, whether I have set about seeking it or have not, whether I have pronounced it or have not, whether I have known it in myself or have not, I ask You by the truth of Your messenger Muhammad and his blessed and noble grave, to graciously attend to all my needs, small and great."

The Master said that whoever is able to remain in the city for three days — Wednesday, Thursday, and Friday — should pray on Wednesday between the grave and the pulpit, at the pillar that adjoins the grave, striving there and asking Allah for his needs in this world and the next. On Thursday he should pray and supplicate likewise at the pillar of repentance, and on Friday he should pray and supplicate at the shrine of the Prophet (ص), opposite the large pillar. He should fast all three days, for in that there is great merit.

The Messenger of Allah (ص) said, as narrated from Ali: "Whoever visits my grave after my death is as one who migrated to me during my life. And whoever cannot visit my grave, let him convey peace upon me, for it will be reported to me."

Jafar ibn Muhammad held that whoever is present in the city should also visit the Mosque of Quba — the mosque founded upon piety — along with al-Fadikh Mosque, al-Fath Mosque, and the Mosque of Umm Ibrahim, praying in each of them, and visiting the grave of Hamza (ع) and the graves of the martyrs, may Allah have mercy upon them. The last act of the pilgrim before leaving Madinah should be to visit the grave of the Prophet (ص) and bid him farewell, saying at that parting what he said at his first arrival.

The Messenger of Allah (ص) fixed the sites of ihram at five stations:

- For the people of Madinah and those allied with them: al-Shajarah Mosque
- For the people of Syria and those beyond them: al-Juhfah
- For the people of Yemen: Yalamlam
- For the people of al-Taif: Qarn
- For the people of Najd: al-Aqiq

Jafar ibn Muhammad said that these are the miqats of ihram for whoever intends to perform Hajj, Umrah, or both together. Ihram must be assumed from the stations the Messenger of Allah (ص) appointed. One must not pass them without entering ihram, and one must not enter ihram before reaching them. Whoever enters ihram before the miqat has departed from the Sunnah, and if he then violates something of his ihram, nothing is required of him — because he was never truly in ihram to begin with, since one does not become a muhrim until he enters ihram from the miqat. He said further that whoever lives closer to Mecca than the miqats should enter ihram from his own home and is not obliged to travel out to the miqat.

He also said, as part of the completion of Hajj and Umrah, that one should enter ihram in the vicinity of one's own people. And he said that whoever fears missing the month for his Umrah may enter ihram before the miqat. Thus, if a man sets out in Rajab intending Umrah but knows he will not reach the miqat until Shaban, he must not delay his ihram until he arrives there — for his Umrah would then become a Shabani Umrah. But if he enters ihram in the remaining days of Rajab, before reaching the miqat, his Umrah becomes a Rajabi Umrah. The Rajabi Umrah is the better of the two, and it was what he had intended from the outset.

The Master also said, concerning one who had passed beyond the tree without entering ihram, that he should enter ihram from whatever point lay between him and al-Juhfah.

He further said that whoever arrives at the miqat and then passes it — whether through forgetfulness, ignorance, or deliberate intent toward Mecca — is in a situation that must be set right:

- If he is able to return to the miqat, he must do so and enter ihram from it.
- If he fears missing the Hajj and cannot return, he enters ihram from wherever he has reached.
- If he is already in Mecca, he must go out to the edge of the Haram and enter ihram from the unrestricted ground, then enter the Haram in a state of ihram.
- If even that is not possible, he enters ihram from where he stands.

At al-Shajarah, during the Farewell Pilgrimage, the Messenger of Allah (ص) commanded those with him to pluck the underarm hair, shave the pubic hair, and remove sewn garments, taking instead a waist-wrapper and a cloak.

It is narrated from Abu Abd Allah Jafar ibn Muhammad that he said the person entering ihram should also, alongside those preparations:

- Use the tooth-stick
- Trim the nails
- Take from the mustache

The order in which these are done carries no consequence.

Ihram is to be entered directly after an obligatory or supererogatory prayer. The preferred time is after the sun has passed its zenith, though no hour is obligatory — ihram may be assumed by day or by night, at any time when prayer is permitted.

He said that a menstruating woman, upon reaching the miqat, performs a ritual bath and enters ihram; and likewise the woman in post-natal bleeding.

He said that a person in ihram must not apply perfume before entering it if the scent will linger on him afterward.

As for a man who enters ihram without having prayed — nothing is required of him for that omission, though entering ihram after a prayer remains the better course.

> أُحِلَّ لَكُمْ صَيْدُ ٱلْبَحْرِ وَطَعَامُهُۥ مَتَعًۭا لَّكُمْ وَلِلسَّيَّارَةِ

When the muhrim wishes to enter ihram, he fixes his intention upon it and, after the prayer, utters aloud what he is binding himself to — whether tamattu', qiran, Hajj alone, or Umrah alone. He says: "O Allah, I intend to enjoy Umrah unto Hajj." Or, if he has a sacrificial animal with him, he says: "O Allah, I intend to join Hajj to Umrah." Or he says: "O Allah, I intend Hajj" or "Umrah," whichever he chooses, and he mentions it upon Your Book and the Sunnah of Your Prophet: "O Allah, my place of release is wherever You detain me, by Your decree which You have decreed upon me. O Allah, help me in that, make it easy for me, and accept it from me." He then supplicates with whatever he wishes. And if he holds the intention in his heart without pronouncing it aloud, that is sufficient and valid.

The Master said that the best form of Hajj is tamattu — Umrah unto Hajj — and that this is the form upon which the Quran was revealed. When the Messenger of Allah (ص) performed the Farewell Hajj, he combined Hajj and Umrah because he had driven a sacrificial animal with him. Upon reaching Mecca, he circumambulated the House and performed the say between Safa and Marwah. Then Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed to him what He revealed, and the Messenger of Allah (ص) said: "Had I foreseen from my affair what I have now seen, I would not have driven the sacrificial animal, and I would have made it a tamattu."

Those who had no sacrificial animal were accordingly to release themselves from ihram, and the people did so, making it an Umrah — all except those who had a sacrificial animal with them. That latter group then entered ihram for Hajj from the Sacred Mosque on the Day of Tarwiyah. Enjoying Umrah unto Hajj is precisely what the Messenger of Allah (ص) commanded, and it accords with what Allah, Mighty and Majestic, said: it is for whoever's family is not present at the Sacred Mosque. As for those who are themselves among the people of the Haram, tamattu is not for them, because they are able to perform Umrah whenever they wish. Allah, Mighty and Majestic, granted this concession only to those who come from other lands, allowing them to perform both Umrah and Hajj within a single journey — a widening from Him and a kindness toward them.

> وَلَا تَحْلِقُوا۟ رُءُوسَكُمْ حَتَّىٰ يَبْلُغَ ٱلْهَدْىُ مَحِلَّهُۥ

Whoever enjoys Umrah unto Hajj, or performs Umrah on its own, enters ihram, then comes to Mecca and circumambulates the House seven circuits, prays the two rakahs of the tawaf, and walks between al-Safa and al-Marwah seven circuits — beginning with al-Safa and ending with al-Marwah — has thereby completed his Umrah. He then releases himself from ihram.

If he is performing tamattu and intends the Hajj, he trims the ends of his hair and nails and remains disheveled, resembling the muhrim, until the Day of Tarwiyah. At that point he enters ihram for the Hajj from the Sacred Mosque, just as he had entered ihram from the miqat.

If, however, he is joining Hajj and Umrah together and has driven the sacrificial animal, he does not release himself but remains in ihram until he completes the Hajj. Allah, Mighty and Majestic, says: ﴿وَلَا تَحْلِقُوا رُءُوسَكُمْ حَتَّىٰ يَبْلُغَ الْهَدْيُ مَحِلَّهُ﴾ — "And do not shave your heads until the sacrificial animal reaches its place of slaughter" [al-Baqarah: 196].

As for whoever performs Hajj alone and comes to Mecca, no tawaf is obligatory upon him until he returns from Arafah. Should he perform a voluntary tawaf in the meantime, that is good and carries merit. And if he proceeds to Mina without entering Mecca at all, there is no harm upon him.

On the marking of the offering and the raising of the voice: Abū Jaʿfar Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī related that in former times people would garland camels, cattle, and sheep, but that the practice of garlanding cattle and sheep was later abandoned. Garlanding, he explained, means tying a strap or cord around an animal driven for Hajj or Umrah; this is done after entering iḥrām at the mīqāt, and the animal is thereby dedicated as an offering. The gashing consists of making a cut on the right side of the animal's hump with a blade until blood flows, so that the animal is recognized as an offering. It is also recommended to hang upon its garland a sandal in which its owner has prayed, so that if the animal strays it will still be known as an offering. Jaʿfar ibn Muḥammad said that the offering is gashed on the right side of its hump, then covered and led on its way.

When one who has entered iḥrām from al-Shajarah reaches al-Bayḍāʾ, he raises his voice with the talbiyah and its announcement, saying: "لَبَّيْكَ اللَّهُمَّ لَبَّيْكَ، لَبَّيْكَ لَا شَرِيكَ لَكَ لَبَّيْكَ، إِنَّ الْحَمْدَ وَالنِّعْمَةَ لَكَ وَالْمُلْكَ، لَا شَرِيكَ لَكَ" — Labbayk Allahumma labbayk, labbayk la sharika laka labbayk, inna al-hamda wa al-nimata laka wa al-mulk, la sharika lak.

He repeats the talbiyah in each of the following circumstances:

- when he ascends a height or descends into a valley
- when he meets a rider
- at the close of every obligatory prayer
- upon waking from sleep
- at the hours before dawn

He may say it whether or not he is in a state of ritual purity, though purity is better, and the more frequently he says it the better. A man raises his voice with the talbiyah, while women keep their voices low, saying it only loud enough to hear themselves.

Whoever must ride his offering animal out of necessity may do so, and whoever needs its milk may drink, provided neither causes the animal harm. As for the offering itself: if it is made under a vow or as an expiation and something befalls it — whether through the owner's fault or not — the obligation holds firm and the owner must replace it. If it is a voluntary offering, nothing further falls upon him.

When a guaranteed offering is slaughtered, its owner does not eat from it; it is given entirely in charity. A voluntary offering, by contrast, may be eaten from, shared with others, and given in charity.

> يَٓأَيُّهَا ٱلَّذِينَ آمَنُوا۟ لَا تَقْتُلُوا۟ ٱلصَّيْدَ وَأَنتُمْ حُرُمٌۭ ۚ وَمَن قَتَلَهُۥ مِنكُم مُّتَعَمِّدًۭا فَجَزَآءٌۭ مِّثْلُ مَا قَتَلَ مِنَ ٱلنَّعَمِ

On what is forbidden to the muhrim and what falls upon him if he commits it, Allah, Mighty and Majestic, said: ﴿الْحَجُّ أَشْهُرٌ مَعْلُومَاتٌ فَمَنْ فَرَضَ فِيهِنَّ الْحَجَّ فَلَا رَفَثَ وَلَا فُسُوقَ وَلَا جِدَالَ فِي الْحَجِّ﴾, "Hajj is in well-known months, and whoever has made the Hajj obligatory upon himself therein, there is no rafath and no transgression and no quarreling during the Hajj" [al-Baqarah: 197]. It has already been established that rafath refers to intercourse.

Allah, Blessed and Exalted, said: ﴿وَلَا تَقْتُلُوا الصَّيْدَ وَأَنتُمْ حُرُمٌ﴾, "And do not kill game while you are in ihram" [al-Maidah: 95], and He whose remembrance is Majestic said: ﴿وَحُرِّمَ عَلَيْكُمْ صَيْدُ الْبَرِّ مَا دُمْتُمْ حُرُمًا﴾, "And forbidden to you is the game of the land as long as you are in ihram" [al-Maidah: 96]. From the People of the House (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ) it is transmitted that the muhrim is barred from four things:

- hunting
- intercourse
- perfume
- wearing sewn garments

The one who offers a sacrificial animal marks it by wounding the right side of its hump with an iron blade until blood flows, so that it is recognized as a sacrificial offering (هَدِي). It is recommended that a sandal in which the offerer has prayed be hung around the animal's collar, so that if it is found straying it will be identified as a hadi. Jafar ibn Muhammad said: the sacrificial camels are marked on the right side of the hump, draped, and driven forward.

At al-Bayda, having entered ihram from the tree, the pilgrim raises the talbiyah and the ihlal, lifting his voice with: لَبَّيْكَ اللَّهُمَّ لَبَّيْكَ، لَبَّيْكَ لَا شَرِيكَ لَكَ لَبَّيْكَ، إِنَّ الْحَمْدَ وَالنِّعْمَةَ لَكَ وَالْمُلْكَ، لَا شَرِيكَ لَكَ. He renews the talbiyah whenever he ascends a height, descends into a valley, or meets a fellow traveler on the road; likewise at the close of every obligatory prayer, upon waking from sleep, and in the hours before dawn — whether he is in a state of ritual purity or not. The more frequently one calls the talbiyah, the better. It is recommended that a man raise his voice with it, and that women keep their voices low, just enough to hear themselves.

A pilgrim who needs to ride his sacrificial animal may do so, and may drink of its milk, provided no harm comes to the animal. Should the animal be harmed — whether through his use of it or for any other reason — the ruling depends on how the offering was originally pledged:

- If it was pledged as a vow or as a compensation, it counts as a guaranteed offering, and he is obliged to furnish a replacement.
- If it was a voluntary offering, nothing is due from him.

When a guaranteed offering is slaughtered, its owner may not eat from it; it must be given entirely in charity. A voluntary offering, by contrast, may be eaten from, shared with others, and given in charity.

On what is forbidden to the one in ihrам and what he owes should he commit it: Allah, Mighty and Majestic, said, ﴿الْحَجُّ أَشْهُرٌ مَعْلُومَاتٌ فَمَنْ فَرَضَ فِيهِنَّ الْحَجَّ فَلَا رَفَثَ وَلَا فُسُوقَ وَلَا جِدَالَ فِي الْحَجِّ﴾ [البقرة: ١٩٧], and it has already been established that rafath means intercourse.

He, Blessed and Exalted, said, ﴿وَلَا تَقْتُلُوا الصَّيْدَ وَأَنتُمْ حُرُمٌ﴾ [المائدة: ٩٥], and He, exalted be His mention, said, ﴿وَحُرِّمَ عَلَيْكُمْ صَيْدُ الْبَرِّ مَا دُمْتُمْ حُرُمًا﴾ [البقرة: ٩٦]. From the Ahl al-Bayt (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ) it is transmitted that the one in ihram is forbidden from hunting, intercourse, perfume, sewn clothing, the removal of hair, and the trimming of nails, along with further matters treated in this chapter and the one that follows it, together with what is owed for each, if Allah the Exalted wills.

Jafar ibn Muhammad said: whoever deliberately has intercourse with his wife while in ihram, knowing it to be unlawful, and reaches her, owes a sacrificial camel. If he had not yet circled the House on the Day of Sacrifice, he must also perform the Hajj again the following year. If the woman consented, knowing it was not lawful for her, she likewise owes a sacrificial camel. If he compelled her, or came to her while she slept, nothing is due from her, and two camels are due from him. If both were ignorant of the prohibition, nothing is due from either; yet whichever of them did know must expiate and repeat the Hajj the following year. If he came to her other than through intercourse, he owes a camel but need not repeat the Hajj. If he acted in forgetfulness, nothing is due from him, and his situation is that of one who ate and drank during the daytime of Ramadan while forgetting.

A man in ihram may not contract a marriage during his ihram, nor conduct the marriage of another. If he does so, the marriage is void. If he knew the act was unlawful, or the woman knew it, she is forever forbidden to him afterward. If both were ignorant, they are separated, and he may marry her once he leaves ihram, should both of them wish it.

The Master said: if a man in ihram is intimate with his wife, or kisses her with desire, or deliberately looks at her with desire, or touches her with desire, and emits, he owes the blood of a sheep or more. If, however, there was neither intent nor desire, and he emits, nothing is due from him.

The Master said: if a man in ihram disputes, swearing "No, by Allah" and "Yes, by Allah," and is truthful in doing so, nothing is due from him — until he does so three times or more, at which point he owes blood. If he was lying, he owes blood for a single instance, and the obligation grows as the instances multiply.

The Master said: if a man in ihram shaves his head, he owes a sheep. If he removes some of the hair of his head or beard, he gives charity in proportion to what he removed. If he merely wipes his head or beard and a small amount falls away on its own, nothing is due from him.

The Master said: if a man in ihram requires cupping, he may be cupped if he wishes. If he shaves the site where the cupping-glasses are applied, he owes a charity. If he clips all his nails, he owes blood; if he clips only some of them, he gives charity in proportion to what he clipped.

A pilgrim in ihram who cannot find sandals may wear leather footwear cut below the ankles. The Master said: if a pilgrim in ihram fears he will be left exposed, he may enter ihram wearing his clothing, and the recompense for that is the blood of a sheep, or charity, or fasting in proportion. A pilgrim in ihram may apply kohl if he needs it, provided it is not antimony and contains no perfume; he may use the tooth-stick if he wishes, and may treat himself with anything otherwise lawful to eat, so long as it carries no perfume. It is disliked for a pilgrim in ihram to shade himself inside the litter except on account of an ailment, though shading himself when he alights is permitted. If a pilgrim in ihram wears clothing or does something otherwise forbidden to him out of ignorance or forgetfulness, nothing is due from him.

There is no harm in entering ihram in a dyed garment, so long as it is not dyed with saffron, wars, or perfume. White is better — it is what is commanded — unless it cannot be found. There is no harm in a woman in ihram wearing ornaments, so long as she does not display them to men. A pilgrim in ihram who is afflicted by something harmful is permitted to remove it.

The man in ihram does not cover his head, nor the woman in ihram her face, though she may let a garment fall over her face without it clinging to it. The man in ihram does not cover his ears. If he suffers a headache, there is no harm in his binding his head, nor in his placing a waterskin upon it when he draws water. If he covers his head, or the woman in ihram covers her face, each owes a charity. There is no harm in a pilgrim in ihram washing, though it is disliked for him to plunge his head into the water.

As for the recompense owed for game taken by a pilgrim in ihram: Allah, Mighty and Majestic, said, ﴿يَا أَيُّهَا الَّذِينَ آمَنُوا لَا تَقْتُلُوا الصَّيْدَ وَأَنتُمْ حُرُمٌ وَمَن قَتَلَهُ مِنكُم مُتَعَمِّدًا فَجَزَاءٌ مِّثْلُ مَا قَتَلَ مِنَ النَّعَمِ﴾ [المائدة: ٩٥]. The Ahl al-Bayt recited it as "one possessing justice among you," in the singular — and that one is the Imam (ع) and whoever the Imam appoints. A lengthy argument surrounds this question, which has been abridged here for the sake of brevity.

Jafar ibn Muhammad said, concerning the words of Allah, Mighty and Majestic, ﴿وَمَنْ عَادَ فَيَنتَقِمُ اللَّهُ مِنْهُ﴾ [المائدة: ٩٥]: whoever kills game while in ihram is judged to give its equivalent; but if he returns and kills again, he is not judged a second time — rather, Allah takes retribution from him directly, for the expiation applies only to the first offense.

He said: whoever takes game while in ihram owes its equivalent in livestock as an offering. If he is performing Hajj, he slaughters the offering at Mina; if he is performing Umrah, he slaughters it at Makkah. Should he find no animal, he gives its price in charity; and if he has nothing to give, he fasts.

He said: the substitutions proceed by degree —

- If a sacrificial camel falls due and he cannot find one, he feeds sixty poor persons; if he has nothing to feed them with, he fasts eighteen days.
- If a cow falls due and he cannot find one, he feeds thirty poor persons; if he cannot, he fasts nine days.
- If a sheep falls due and he cannot find one, he gives charity to ten poor persons; if he cannot, he fasts three days.

He said: whoever can find neither an offering nor the means to give charity should begin fasting. If he then finds the means during the fast, he may continue fasting if he wishes, and it suffices him.

For the one in ihram who takes an ostrich or a wild ass, he said, a sacrificial camel is due for each. For the wild cow, a domestic cow is due; and for the gazelle, the dove, and birds of their kind, a sheep for each.

If he takes the eggs of an ostrich, the stallion is set upon a number of she-camels equal to the number of eggs. Whatever those she-camels bring forth he offers as expiation; for whatever is not brought forth, nothing is due — for among eggs there are always those that do not come to term.

If he breaks an egg and finds chicks within it, he must set the stallion upon the she-camels until they conceive and their burden is plain. Whatever they then bring forth he offers; for whatever miscarries, nothing is due — for among chicks there are those that die before hatching.

If he takes chicks that have quickened and in which the spirit has already arisen, he sets the stallion upon an equal number of she-camels until they conceive and their young stir in the womb. Whatever they bring forth after that he offers; for whatever miscarries or dies, nothing is due from him — for a chick in the egg, even after the spirit has arisen within it, may still die.

This ruling on the eggs taken by one in ihram has been transmitted only through the Imams of the Ahl al-Bayt.

An Arab came to Umar (رض) during his caliphate and said: "O Commander of the Faithful, I set out for Hajj and entered ihram, then passed by a nesting-place — the spot where the ostrich gathers its eggs and secures them — and I took some of those eggs, roasted them, and ate them. What do you judge for me in that?"

Umar said: "Go to your companions. Whatever they judge for you, I will confirm." He gestured toward a group of the Companions of the Messenger of Allah (ص) who were seated in the mosque.

The man went to them and put his question, and they disagreed among themselves. He returned to Umar and said: "There is a man among you to whom, whenever a matter fell into dispute, you would turn for judgment. Send me to him."

Umar asked: "And who is he?"

The man said: "Ali ibn Abi Talib."

The man went and found Ali in the company of al-Hasan and al-Husayn (عَلَيْهِمَا السَّلَامُ). He told him the story and asked for the ruling. Ali said to him: "Ask these two" — and he gestured toward al-Hasan and al-Husayn (عَلَيْهِمَا السَّلَامُ).

The Arab said: "What is this I keep meeting from you, O Companions of Muhammad? Every time I ask one of you, he sends me on to another, until now you have turned me back to the children." He rose to leave.

Some of those present said: "These two are the sons of the Messenger of Allah (ص). Ask them, O Arab — with them you will find what you seek."

So he asked them, and they said: "Set a stallion of the camels upon young she-camels equal in number to the eggs you took, and whatever is brought forth from that, you offer."

The Arab put the question to Ali: was it so? Ali said: "Yes, O Arab." The man departed. Umar saw him and sent for him, asking what had passed, and the man told him. Umar said: "Return to him and say that the young she-camels may miscarry — that is, cast their young before birth — or may not conceive at all." He returned with this, and they said to him: "And likewise the eggs may be addled — that is, spoiled by brooding so that no chicks come of them." He went back to Umar and reported this, and Umar said: "For this very reason we were commanded to ask them." In another account, Umar himself put the question to them directly.

The ruling concerning chicks that are struck — counted according to those living and those dead — is traced to Jafar ibn Muhammad (عَلَيْهِمَا السَّلَامُ) on the same principle that came from Ali. He said that the one in a state of ihram who takes a wild cow owes a domestic cow in recompense; if he cannot find one, he feeds thirty poor persons; and if he cannot do that, he fasts nine days. They also said that for a fawn taken by one in ihram, a sheep is owed; if he cannot provide a sheep, he feeds ten poor persons; and if he cannot, he fasts three days. The same obligation falls upon everyone for whom a sheep becomes due as recompense for game, and what is owed in that regard will be set out in due course.

Whoever owes a recompense but can find neither the equivalent animal, nor the means to feed the poor, nor the ability to fast, nothing further is due from him. Allah, Mighty and Majestic, says, ﴿لَا يُكَلِّفُ اللَّهُ نَفْسًا إِلَّا وُسْعَهَا﴾ [البقرة: ٢٨٦], and He says, ﴿لَا يُكَلِّفُ اللَّهُ نَفْسًا إِلَّا مَا آتَاهَا﴾ [الطلاق: ٧]. Whoever neither possesses nor has within his power what he is charged with owes nothing until he acquires it or becomes capable of it. If the one upon whom such an obligation fell then comes into some portion of what is due, he fulfills it; and if he becomes able to fast, he fasts, as has been stated.

Concerning the one in ihram who takes a hyena, a fox, or a hare, the scholars held that a sheep is due for each, or its equivalent in feeding or fasting if he cannot provide the sheep, as described above. This penalty was laid upon him so that he would shun hunting.

They also held that for the dove and birds of its kind — the wood-pigeon, the turtle-dove, the partridge, and whatever resembles them among birds — a sheep is due for each one, or its equivalent in feeding and fasting if a sheep cannot be provided. If he takes the egg of any of these birds, he does with the sheep as the one who takes the ostrich's eggs does with the camels, as has already been said.

For one in ihram who takes a monitor lizard, a jerboa, or any similar creature, a young kid is due for each. For one in ihram who takes game in unconsecrated land, the recompense is as already described; but if he takes it within the Sacred Precinct, both that recompense and the value of the game are due together, the value given in charity — a severity and an emphasis, magnifying the Precinct, so that nothing Allah, Mighty and Majestic, and His Messenger (ص) have forbidden is held as trifling.

Where a single game animal is taken by a company of people in ihram, each of them severally bears the full recompense, for each has individually transgressed. The one in ihram is forbidden to direct another toward game, to point it out, or to assist in its taking. He is, however, permitted to eat of game when he is in dire need, and that suffices him; he does not resort to carrion while game is available to him.

If one in ihram strikes at game and wounds it, or breaks its foreleg or hindleg, and it then flees and is lost to him so that he does not know what became of it, the full recompense is due from him. If, on the other hand, he recovers the animal and finds it sound and releases it, a quarter of the recompense is due for the breaking of its foreleg or hindleg, and for a wound, a portion of the recompense proportionate to the share that wound would bear of the blood-money — given in charity.

When one in ihram slaughters game as his recompense, he may not eat of it, nor may he feed it to anyone else — whether that person is in the unconsecrated state or in ihram. If he does so, the recompense falls due a second time. The scholars ruled that it be buried so that no one eats of it. If it is dug up and eaten after he has buried it, nothing further is owed from him, provided he neither ordered that nor pointed it out. Any liability in that case falls upon the one who took it.

On the question of one in ihram who kills game by mistake or in ignorance, the scholars held that the full recompense is due from him, exactly as it is from one who did so deliberately. This is very nearly the consensus of the Muslims, and most of them hold it. Allah, Mighty and Majestic, says: ﴿يَا أَيُّهَا الَّذِينَ آمَنُوا لَا تَقْتُلُوا الصَّيْدَ وَأَنتُمْ حُرُمٌ وَمَن قَتَلَهُ مِنكُم مُتَعَمِّدًا فَجَزَاءٌ مِّثْلُ مَا قَتَلَ مِنَ النَّعَمِ﴾ [المائدة: ٩٥], and the Friends of Allah (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ) know best the interpretation of His Book, Mighty and Majestic. Nothing in that verse, nor anything on the tongue of the Messenger of Allah (ص), makes plain that the recompense is lifted from one in ihram who kills game by mistake.

As for the saying of the Messenger of Allah (ص), "Allah has lifted from my community their mistakes, their forgetfulness, and what they are compelled to" — this does not bear on the present question. What he (ص) meant is that Allah has lifted from them the sin attached to what they did not intend. For he (ص) himself made blood-money obligatory in cases of mistaken killing.

The penalty owed for wounds inflicted upon a sane person rests upon its own principle, and that same principle governs the one in ihram who kills game. The penalty is not waived when a person in ihram kills game by accident, for nothing in either the Book or the Sunnah removes it from the one who committed the act. Its basis is the words of Allah the Almighty: "O you who believe, Allah will test you with something of the game that your hands and your spears reach" [Al-Maidah: 94]. The verse addresses those in ihram, and whatever a person in ihram takes — whether by hand or by weapon — falls within what Allah the Almighty has set before them here. What is established from the Imams (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ) is binding, because Allah the Almighty has joined obedience to them with obedience to Him and to His Messenger (ص); and what is established from the Messenger of Allah (ص) is a proof for whoever accepts it, as is the consensus of the Ummah. The full argument on this point is lengthy, and what has been cited here suffices for one who consents to receive the truth, God willing.

If a person in ihram catches a fish within the sacred precinct, he must release it. Likewise, if he comes to possess game that was taken from the haram, he must return it there. For each small bird he kills — larks, sparrows, swifts, and their like — he owes a mudd of food. For a locust, or whatever resembles or approaches it such as wasps, bees, and the like, a handful of food is owed, provided the killing was deliberate. Whatever lands on him unbidden, however, or whatever his mount tramples without his intention, incurs no penalty. The same ruling applies to the creeping things of the earth: beetles and their like, and similarly ants, corn weevils, flies, mosquitoes, ticks, and lice.

The person in ihram is permitted to kill whatever harms or endangers him: harmful flies, scorpions, snakes, birds of prey, the four-footed vermin — the mouse and the biting dog — and whatever falls into that same kind and runs the same course. No penalty attaches to him for killing any of these. He is likewise permitted to drive off the crow and the kite. The catch of the sea is lawful for him, as God the Almighty has plainly permitted it in His Book, so that he may take it, eat of it, and provision himself from it.

Regarding water birds and marsh birds, the scholars held the following:

- Whatever lays its eggs on land belongs to the game of the land.
- Whatever lays its eggs in the water belongs to the catch of the sea.

They also held that nothing counts as game among birds except what takes independently to flight. Domestic fowl and their like — birds that dwell among buildings and houses, such as the Sindi hen and its kind — are not game at all.

Finally, whoever incurs a penalty for game slaughters it at Mina if he is performing Hajj, or at Makkah if he is performing Umrah.

> أَوَلَمْ يَرَوْا۟ أَنَّا جَعَلْنَا حَرَمًا آمِنًۭا

وَيُسَبِّحُ الرَّعْدُ بِحَمْدِهِ وَالْمَلَـٰٓئِكَةُ مِنْ خِيفَتِهِ — the verse the preceding passage closed on opens onto what follows.

As for the sacred precinct, its sanctity, its entry, and what is permitted within it: Allah the Almighty said, أَوَلَمْ يَرَوْا۟ أَنَّا جَعَلْنَا حَرَمًا آمِنًۭا — "Have they not seen that We have made a safe sanctuary?" [Al-Ankabut: 67]. It is reported from the Messenger of Allah (ص) that he forbade anyone to disturb the game of the haram or to cut its trees.

On the day of the conquest of Makkah, he (ص) stood with his back to the House, holding the doorpost, while the people of Makkah and others crowded before him, and he proclaimed: "There is no god but Allah, alone, with no partner. He fulfilled His promise, granted victory to His servant, and defeated the confederates alone. What do you say, O people of Makkah, and what do you expect?"

They answered: "We say good, and we think well: a noble brother and the son of a noble brother, and you have power."

He said: "I say as my brother Yusuf said: No blame upon you today; may Allah forgive you, and He is the most merciful of the merciful. Yet Allah the Almighty made Makkah sacred on the day He created the heavens and the earth, and it remains forbidden until the Day of Resurrection. Its game shall not be disturbed, its trees shall not be cut, its thorny shrubs shall not be uprooted, and its lost property shall not be taken except by one who will announce it."

Al-Abbas said: "O Messenger of Allah, save for idhkhir, for it serves the graves and the houses."

What Al-Abbas meant was that idhkhir would be laid over the dead in their graves as a guard against the earth, placed beneath them as a lining, set upon the roofs of houses, and burned by goldsmiths as fuel.

The Messenger of Allah (ص) said, "Save for idhkhir." He spoke much else at that place, and by these words the sanctity of the haram is defined and preserved.

He (ص) commanded that none of the haram's trees be felled and that its game not be startled in violation of its sanctity. What is genuinely needed, however, is permitted — such as:

- a staff for the shepherd, or what makes his water-bucket serviceable
- fruits
- trees that grow into people's dwellings
- what beasts and camels graze with their mouths
- what people themselves plant, as distinct from what the haram brings forth on its own

Whoever takes anything beyond that must give its value in charity.

Anyone entering the haram is recommended to perform a ritual bath beforehand. One who joins Umrah to Hajj cuts off the talbiyah upon sighting the houses of Makkah, and then calls out the takbir and the tahlil. In Hajj, the talbiyah is cut off when the sun declines on the Day of Arafah; in a separate Umrah, when the pilgrim enters the haram.

When the hajj pilgrim, the umrah performer, the qarin, or the mutamatti enters Makkah, the first order of business — if he has a mount and gear — is to secure his baggage and safeguard his belongings. Only then does he go out, occupying himself with nothing else, and make straight for the Sacred Mosque. He approaches it with tranquility and dignity, holding in his heart the exaltation of the House. Walking to it barefoot is best, though wearing sandals or riding does no harm. It is recommended that he enter by the Gate of Bani Shayba — known as the Gate of the Iraqis — and that he supplicate with whatever comes readily to him.

Among the supplications transmitted for that moment is one from Jafar ibn Muhammad (صَلَوَاتُ اللَّهِ عَلَيْهِ), who commanded: "When you enter Makkah and set down your baggage, begin with the Sacred Mosque. When you reach it, stand at the gate facing the Stone and say: In the name of Allah, and by Allah, and to Allah, and as Allah wills, and upon the creed of the Messenger of Allah (ص). The finest of names belong to Allah, and praise be to Allah, and peace be upon the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon Muhammad ibn Abdillah, peace be upon you, O Prophet, and the mercy of Allah and His blessings. Peace be upon the prophets of Allah and His messengers. Peace be upon Ibrahim and Ismail. Peace be upon the messengers and upon the righteous servants of Allah. O Allah, send blessings upon Muhammad and the family of Muhammad, and have mercy upon Muhammad and the family of Muhammad, and bless Muhammad and the family of Muhammad, as You blessed and had mercy upon and blessed Ibrahim and the family of Ibrahim among the worlds; surely You are Praiseworthy, Glorious. O Allah, send blessings upon Muhammad, Your servant and Your Messenger, and send blessings upon Ibrahim, Your intimate friend, and upon Your prophets and messengers. Open for me the gates of Your mercy, employ me in Your obedience and in what pleases You, and preserve me with the safekeeping of faith so long as You keep me alive. Exalted be the praise of Your Face. Praise be to Allah who has made me among His delegation and among the visitors of His House.

Upon every place that is visited, and upon every one who is visited there, the visitor owes a due; and Allah is the best of those visited and the best of those come to. The prayer continues:

"So I ask You by Allah, O Most Merciful — by the fact that You are Allah, there is no god but You alone, with no partner, and that Muhammad is Your servant and Messenger, and that You are One, Eternal, who did not beget and was not begotten, and to whom none is equal: O Bountiful, O Majestic, O Tender, O Generous, make Your gift to me, and let the first thing You grant me be my release from the Fire. O Allah, free my neck from the Fire."

This is said three times, then:

"And widen for me lawful, wholesome provision, and ward off from me the evil of the devils of jinn and men, and the evil of the corrupt among Arab and non-Arab."

The pilgrim then draws near the House, raises his hands, and says:

"O Allah, I ask You, in this my standing, at the beginning of my rites, that You accept my repentance, that You pass over my offense, and that You lift from me my burden. Praise be to Allah who has brought me to His Sacred House."

He then advances to the corner and kisses the Black Stone if he is able and it is within reach, or touches it — which is to lay his hands upon it. If he cannot reach it, he points toward it with his hand. He then takes up the tawaf.

> ثُمَّ لْيَقْضُوا۟ تَفَثَهُمْ وَلْيُوفُوا۟ نُذُورَهُمْ وَلْيَطَّوَّفُوا۟ بِٱلْبَيْتِ ٱلْعَتِيقِ

Concerning the tawaf, Allah the Almighty said, "And let them circumambulate the Ancient House" [Al-Hajj: 29], and He, Blessed and Exalted, said, "Purify My House for those who circumambulate, those who abide there, and those who bow and prostrate" [Al-Baqarah: 125].

Abu Jafar Muhammad ibn Ali (صَلَوَاتُ اللَّهِ عَلَيْهِ) was asked about the origin of the tawaf. He said that when Allah the Almighty created Adam, He commanded the angels to prostrate to him and honored him by teaching him the names. The angels grew proud within themselves and said, "We did not think that Allah the Almighty would create a creature more honored in His sight than we." When He informed them that He would settle Adam upon the earth, they said, "Will You place therein one who will spread corruption in it and shed blood?" — and in this they raised an objection before Allah the Almighty.

He, Blessed and Exalted, said to them, "Did I not tell you that I know the unseen of the heavens and the earth, and I know what you reveal" — meaning the speech they had uttered — "and what you were concealing" [Al-Baqarah: 33] — meaning what they had held within themselves and counted great concerning the affair of Adam.

When they understood that they had fallen into offense, they took refuge at the Throne and circled it seven times, seeking their Lord's pleasure. He was pleased with them and said, "Descend and build for Me a house upon the earth, that whoever of My servants disobeys Me may circle it, so that I may be pleased with them as I have been pleased with you." So they built a house at the place of the House.

Adam was the first to circle it, and so it remained until the House was raised up at the time of the Flood. Then Allah the Almighty revealed to Ibrahim, and he rebuilt the House upon its original foundation.

Abbad ibn Bakr al-Basri said to Jafar ibn Muhammad (صَلَوَاتُ اللَّهِ عَلَيْهِ), "O son of the Messenger of Allah, a report from the Messenger of Allah (ص) has reached us, and we wish to lay it before you: if it is true in your sight we will accept it, and if it is false we will leave it."

Jafar asked, "What is it?"

Abbad ibn Bakr said, "It has reached us that he (ص) said: Whoever rises after the first part of the night has passed, completes his ablution, touches the Stone, testifies that there is no god but Allah and that Muhammad is the Messenger of Allah, then circles seven times and prays two rakahs — reciting, after the Opening of the Book, *Say, O disbelievers* and *Say, He is Allah, One* — in whichever part of the mosque he wishes, then stands between the Stone and the door and praises Allah the Almighty as He deserves and sends blessings upon me and upon the people of my House: his feet shall not stir until he is granted his request, eight gates of Paradise are opened for him to enter by whichever he wishes, and Allah the Almighty writes for him three thousand good deeds, effaces from him three thousand evil deeds, and raises him three thousand degrees."

Abu Abdillah (عَلَيْهِ السَّلَامُ) said, "All of that is true and sound — and it is six thousand."

When the one performing tawaf begins at the House and kisses the Black Stone — or touches it, or points toward it — he takes up the circling. He moves to his right, keeping the House on his left, and circles seven times, starting at the Black Corner and ending there. Each time he sets out and returns to that corner completes one circuit, and so it continues until all seven are done: three at a brisk pace and four at a walking pace. The brisk pace is a swift manner of walking. The Messenger of Allah (ص) performed it so and commanded his companions to do the same, and they did.

The reason had reached him: Quraysh were saying that the companions of Muhammad had grown weak and that the fever of Yathrib had worn them down. He wished to show them the hardiness and strength of his companions. If the one performing tawaf omits the brisk pace in those three circuits, or in some of them, nothing is held against him. Women are not required to observe the brisk pace at all.

In every tawaf of the House, the one performing it kisses the Black Corner and the Yemeni Corner, or touches them if he is able, or points to them if he cannot. He circles from behind the Hijr and does not enter it; if out of ignorance he does enter it, he comes out and resumes his tawaf from behind it. The two corners that adjoin the Hijr are not to be touched. Whoever cannot circle on foot is carried and circled while borne. Circling barefoot is better, though there is no harm in circling in sandals. Recitation and supplication are recommended throughout the tawaf, and permitted speech during it carries no objection.

Among what has been narrated from Jafar ibn Muhammad (صَلَوَاتُ اللَّهِ عَلَيْهِ) as supplication during the tawaf, he directed that when one circles the House and comes opposite the waterspout, one says, "O Allah, free me from the Fire"; and when one comes opposite the Black Stone, one says, "O Allah, grant me good in this world and good in the Hereafter, and guard me from the torment of the Fire."

When one reaches the seventh circuit and arrives at the Stone, one clings to the House, pressing belly and face against it, and says: "Praise be to Allah who has honored you, ennobled you, and magnified you, and made you a place of return for people and a sanctuary. O Allah, the House is Your House and the servant is Your servant, and this is the standing-place of one who seeks refuge with You from the Fire. O Allah, I am poor before You, fearful of You, and seeking Your protection. Do not alter my body and do not change my name. O Allah, I am weak, so strengthen my weakness in Your good pleasure, and multiply my deed and accept it from me. O Allah, grant me good in this world and good in the Hereafter, and guard us from the torment of the Fire."

He said that angels are present there and say "Amen" to the supplication of the believer.

One performing tawaf must do so only in a state of ritual purity, with ablution. If the obligatory tawaf is performed without ablution, it must be repeated in full. If a voluntary tawaf is performed without ablution, he makes ablution afterward and prays the two rakahs of tawaf, and nothing further is required of him.

Should his ablution break, or should his nose bleed, during the tawaf, he makes ablution or cleanses the blood, then resumes from where he left off — provided the tawaf is voluntary. If it is obligatory and he had completed more than half the circuits, he likewise resumes; but if he had completed half or fewer, he begins the tawaf again from the start.

The obligatory tawaf for the mutamatti and the qarin consists of two parts: first, the tawaf of Umrah, performed upon entering Makkah; then, the tawaf of Hajj upon returning from Mina, which is also the tawaf of ifadah. Each of these is accompanied by a say between al-Safa and al-Marwah, the manner of which will be described in its proper place. Following the tawaf of ifadah and its say comes the tawaf of the women, which is a circling of the House alone. Every obligatory tawaf consists of seven circuits. The tawaf of farewell, performed upon departing Makkah, is recommended and strongly encouraged. Beyond these lie the voluntary tawafs, which carry their own merit: one may circle as often as one wishes, by night or by day, provided each session consists of no fewer than seven circuits.

The Master said that angels are present there and say "Amen" to the prayer of the believer.

Tawaf may never be performed without ritual purity; ablution is required. If a person performs the obligatory tawaf without ablution, he must repeat it entirely. If he performs a voluntary tawaf without ablution, there are no two concluding rakahs for him to pray, and nothing further is required of him.

Should his ablution break during the tawaf — whether through a discharge or otherwise — he performs ablution, washes the affected place, and then continues. If the tawaf was voluntary, he builds upon what he has already completed, even if the interruption came at the very beginning. If it was the obligatory tawaf, the rule depends on how far he had progressed: if he had passed the halfway point, he builds upon it; if he had completed only half or less, he begins again from the start.

The obligatory tawaf takes several forms:

- The tawaf of Umrah, performed upon first entering Makkah.
- The tawaf of Hajj — the tawaf al-Ifadah — performed on returning from Mina.

Each of these is followed by a sai between al-Safa and al-Marwah, which will be described in its proper place. After the tawaf al-Ifadah and its sai comes the tawaf of women, which is a circling of the House alone. Every obligatory tawaf consists of seven circuits. The tawaf of farewell, performed upon departing Makkah, is recommended and strongly encouraged. Beyond these lie the voluntary tawafs, which carry their own merit: one may circle as often as one wishes, by night or by day, provided each session consists of no fewer than seven circuits. The Master said that angels are present there and say "Amen" to the prayer of the believer.

The Master said: A menstruating woman and a woman in a state of lochia complete all the rites once they have washed and purified themselves, with one exception — the tawaf. She does not perform it until she is pure. A woman with irregular bleeding may circumambulate and complete all the rites, provided she is not in a confirmed state of menstruation. A menstruating woman may perform the sa'i between al-Safa and al-Marwah once she has purified herself, and she then makes up both the tawaf and the sa'i. Women who fear the onset of menstruation should hasten their tawaf and their visit.
